# DOC_SURGERY -- every document checked against the interpreter, 2026-10-07

**The author's ask (2026-10-07):** *"update the documents where they need updated, and serious surgery needs to be
done with the documents, we gotta tear out all of the old stuff, and update stuff depending upon the state of the
satl interpreter, so much does not apply anymore, and some files may be able to just be deleted altogether, but we
have to be really careful... If your gonna delete an entire file, ask me first... Everything needs to be checked"*.

**How it was checked:** 33 fresh readers, one per document or small group (24) and nine for the 85 ERRORS4 bug
tickets, each against the INSTALLED satl (`~/.satl/satl`, VERSION 004 REVISION 09 BUILD 0054) and the source --
never a document's own BUILT mark. 682 tool uses, 3.4 M tokens. The per-reader JSON is in the session's workflow
journal (wf_c7700130-9e9); this file is the rendering. SATELLITE_FILE_OPERATIONS.md was NOT assessed: another
session is rewriting it (FO-5..FO-10).

**Verdict words:** keep = true as it stands; update = right in the main, named corrections; gut = mostly history or
wrong today, keep a short record plus the author's words and every unanswered question; delete = nothing of its own
left (the author decides every delete); ask = his call either way.

**Headline findings:**
- 78 of the 85 bugs found on 2026-09-26 (ERRORS4) are STILL THERE on build 0054; 3 are fixed (046, 047, 082) and 4
  changed shape (012, 036, 045, 075). ERRORS4/ is live, not history.
- The big plan documents (PLAN, MILESTONES, SATELLITE_WINDOW, SATELLITE_ARGUMENTS, GTK_AND_NO_DEPENDENCIES, ERROR,
  CPU-PLAN) are 80-90% history, each with a few live items and the author's words that must survive.
- NEW_MILESTONES.md, MISSING_SYNTAX.md, SATELLITE_INFINITY.md, THREADS.md, ERRORS3.md, LANGUAGE_IN_SATELLITE.md and
  the POLYMORPH notes are live plans with bounded corrections.
- One doc is proposed for deletion (errors.md: all 5 items fixed and recorded elsewhere) and one is his to answer
  (SCRATCH.md/INCOMPLETE.md). SCRATCH.md/PLAN_DOCS_STATUS.md (2026-10-06, build 0043) is superseded by this file.

---

## SCRATCH.md/ERRORS4/ -- the 85 bug tickets of 2026-09-26, re-run on BUILD 0054

{'still-there': 78, 'changed': 4, 'fixed': 3}

### fixed (3)

- **046** 046-item-method-refusal-late-and-misnamed.md: Program ran: 'before' then '3' (names[1].size on {"zoe","al"}), exit 0. The page's wrong refusal ('names.size is not built for a string yet', run-time S210) no longer appears because a string's .size is now built (string help line 46: '.size is how many characters the string holds'). Variant names[1].frobnicate is refused by satl(check) S201, exit 25, before 'before' prints, as the page said it sh _The underlying misnaming path (expression.cpp head-name in the refusal) was not exercised by this program any more; a not-yet-built string method on an item would be needed to tell. t046.satl, t046v.s_
- **047** 047-string-index-s301-at-run.md: 'before' then 'a' printed (s[1] on "abc"), exit 0: reading one character with s[n] is now built, and the string help (line 52) now documents s.at(1) and s[s.size]. Page expected a check-time S210; the feature being built supersedes that. Variant s[1] = "z" is now refused at satl(check) time (S301 'in satellite.main, s is a string, and [ ] = ... changes an item of a list or a key of an index', exit _t047.satl, t047v.satl + .out._
- **082** 082-literal-replace-refused-late.md: rp1.satl: exit 0, prints 'before' then 'bbc' -- "abc".replace("a","b") on a literal is now BUILT and runs; rp2.satl (s.replace on a declared name) also exit 0, 'bbc'. Page expected a check-time S210 before 'before' printed; there is no S210 at all now. S210 still exists (s_codes.hpp:65). _The premise is gone rather than the ordering repaired: string.replace is built, so the late-vs-early refusal inconsistency cannot occur. No ticket-shaped wrong behaviour remains._

## Each document

### errors.md -- DELETE (high)

**What it is:** An eight-line note of five known bugs, addressed to the author ('Following your no-limits rule', 'after your "just do it all for them" ruling'); SCRATCH.md/NEW_ERROR_LIST.md:14 calls them 'your five items in errors.md'.

**Why:** All five claims are false today, each settled by a run of build 0054, and each is already recorded as fixed with evidence in SCRATCH.md/NEW_ERROR_LIST.md Part D (lines 213-217) and SCRATCH.md/PLAN_DOCS_STATUS.md:240-245. Nothing in it is a ruling or a measurement that lives nowhere else; the two author phrases it quotes ('no-limits rule', 'just do it all for them') are quoted in other documents. The author decides the delete; if he wants a trace, one line in ERROR.md's Fixed section ('errors.md's five items, all fixed by 2026-10-07') is enough.

**Wrong today:**

- line 1: says _satellite.return inside an if doesn't end the capsule, and inside a loop it only ends that pass._ -- **satellite.return() inside an if ends the capsule; inside a while it ends the capsule, not the pass** (run: p12_return_in_if2.satl -> 'returned inside if' (not 'after the if'), then 'pass' once (not 'after the while'), then 'back in main', exit 0)
- line 3: says _A capsule calling itself crashes at about 5,000 deep. Following your no-limits rule, the fix is the walker keeping its own stack, not a cap._ -- **50,000 mid-body self-calls run in 0.35 s; the fix built was stack segments (a fresh 256 MiB segment under a megabyte of headroom), not a walker-owned stack** (run: p2_recursion.satl down(50000) -> 'reached the bottom' 'back in main', exit 0, real 0.351s; satellite/machine/stack_segments.hpp:1-3; program_walk.cpp:2401; tests/recursion_in_the_middle.satl (10,000, in check.sh's tests))
- line 5: says _satellite.include(./x) without quotes includes nothing, and nothing says so._ -- **./x is an include spelling; a missing file is refused by name** (run: p3_include_noquotes.satl -> '[satellite] cannot locate file: .../error/./x.satl, included by .../p3_include_noquotes.satl (machine_code: 8 missing_satl_file)', exit 8; satellite/bytecode/include_shape.cpp:90 'AND WITH . OR .. -- include(./parts/)
- line 7: says _A statement outside every capsule is silently skipped._ -- **Refused before anything runs** (run: p4_outside.satl -> 'S110: LINE_NOT_UNDERSTOOD satl(check): this line is outside every capsule, and nothing outside a capsule ever runs -- there are no globals in satellite', exit 13 ('ran' never printed))
- line 9: says _"n = " + 4 is refused in 004. 003 joined it after your "just do it all for them" ruling, and that was never carried over._ -- **It is joined: both an assignment and a direct display of "n = " + 4 print 'n = 4'** (run: p5_join.satl -> 'n = 4' 'n = 4', exit 0)

**Must survive:** Nothing unique. The two author phrases quoted -- 'your no-limits rule' and 'your "just do it all for them" ruling' -- are references, not the rulings themselves (NEW_ERROR_LIST.md:49-63 A1 and :119-127 A8 quote the same items with the author's options). If the author wants the trace kept: the exact five lines, dated, under ERROR.md's Fixed section.

**Edit plan:** Delete the file (the author's call). If kept instead: replace its body with one dated line per item in the form 'FIXED <date>: <the original sentence>' and move it under ERROR.md's Fixed section as 'errors.md's five items', since ERROR.md's own rule is 'Never delete one'. README.md does not link errors.md (grep README.md -> only ERROR.md at :103), so nothing else points at it.

**Overlaps:** SCRATCH.md/NEW_ERROR_LIST.md:14 and Part D lines 213-217 (each of the five with its status), :49-63 (A1, the "n = " + 4 question with the author's two options), :119-127 (A8, recursion); SCRATCH.md/PLAN_DOCS_STATUS.md:240-245 (all five marked FIXED with runs on build 0043); ERROR.md's walker section and Fixed item 1 cover items 2 and 4.

**Not checked:** Who wrote the file (the 'your' reads as Claude addressing the author; NEW_ERROR_LIST.md:14 calls them the author's items)

### SCRATCH.md/INCOMPLETE.md -- ASK (high)

**What it is:** A 13-line question from the author (commit 39fcd8d, 2026-10-02: 'SCRATCH.md/INCOMPLETE.md: his question, not answered here'): is `satellite.container.map<type1, type2> my_map` then `my_map.append({type1_object, type2_object})` accepted, and does `my_map[different_type1_object]` display the type2_object?

**Why:** The question has a definite answer today -- the declaration and `my_map[key]` work, `my_map.append({k, v})` is refused on purpose (S301 'an index has keys and not positions: write my_map[key] = value', a refusal satellite.test checks for) -- but the file is a question of HIS, and only he knows whether it was 'does this work?' (answered: no; delete or fold into the map help, which already says so) or 'make .append({k, v}) a second spelling' (a live wish that belongs in a plan). An unanswered question of his does not become decided by a probe.

**Must survive:** His question verbatim, lines 6-13: *"is this syntax accepted?: satellite.container.map<type1, type2> my_map / my_map.append({type1_object, type2_object}) / satellite.console.display(my_map[different_type1_object]) / should display the type2_object"* -- and beside it today's answer with the probe: declaration accepted; `.append({"a", 1})` refused S301 exit 27 ('in my_map, my_map.append -- an index has keys and not positions: write my_map[key] = value'); `my_map["a"] = 1` then `my_map["a"]` prints 1; `my_map.set("a", 1)` also works.

**Edit plan:** If he says the question is answered: delete the file (the refusal and the right spelling are in satellite.help/satellite.container.map/help_text.txt:29 '.append .insert .remove_at .truncate .index_of and .search are refused, because a map has keys and not positions', in satellite/bytecode/container_calls.cpp:406, and tested in satellite/satellite_test/programs/manifest.tsv:125). If he wants `.append({key, value})` to work: move his four lines verbatim into the open-questions section of the map plan (SCRATCH.md/CONTAINERS.md or MAP_SORT.md's overrulable list), headed 'his question, 2026-10-02, not built', with the one-line answer above, and delete this file. Either way add the date 2026-10-02 (the file carries none).

**Overlaps:** satellite.help/satellite.container.map/help_text.txt:14-16 and :29 (writes are `age[k] = v`, .append refused by design); satellite/bytecode/container_calls.cpp:406 (the S301 text); satellite/satellite_test/programs/manifest.tsv:125-126 and test_programs.cpp:4947 (the refusal is a checked expectation of satellite.test); check.sh:911-950 map rows. SCRATCH.md/CONTAINERS.md and MAP_SORT.md are the map plan documents.

### CPU-PLAN.md -- GUT (medium)

**What it is:** The 2026-09-23 plan for one satl with four big-number kernels inside (CPU-1..CPU-5), plus two of the author's additions (CPU-6 names ahead of time, CPU-7 PGO/LTO/BOLT), the measurements of 2026-09-23 behind it, and -- in its own header -- the author's ruling that set the kernel plan aside the same day.

**Why:** By the author's own ruling in the header ("let's just scrap this whole CPU thing") the kernel plan (§1-§7, CPU-1..CPU-5, the Strings kernel interface, CPU-7 'What was planned') is history: no kernel, no --bit (satl --bit exits 23 command_line_not_understood), no kernel line in the banner, cpu_level.cpp still a standalone build/satl-cpu-level (055-cpus.mk; not in 040-sources.mk). Several facts are wrong today (62 word .so libraries -> words compiled in since 2026-10-07; .find/.replace 'not built' -> built; profile 'regenerated every build' -> made once and kept; source line references moved). What is live and recorded NOWHERE else: the three author quotes, every measurement of 2026-09-23 (28.6% names, 1.6 ns vs 38-46 ns, 8.5x memcmp, GMP 1.50x, PGO 10.5-13.0%), CPU-6 (still not built: expression.cpp:1486 text_at rebuilds the name, :1501 context.variables.seen(name) hashes it, value.hpp:115 VariableTable is unordered_map), the string == memcmp recommendation (still char_traits<char16_t>::compare at satellite_string.cpp:283, == at satellite_string.hpp:196), the open decision on make cpus/build/cpu (53 folders, Sep 23, still there), and CPU-7's race table (PROGRESS.md section 1 has no PGO row; grep PGO PROGRESS.md -> nothing). Gut to a short record carrying those; the author may instead want the shelved kernel plan intact in case he returns to it -- that is his call.

**Wrong today:**

- line 6 (header) and CPU-7 lines 334-343: 'does all three on every make: 1. It builds satl a second time, instrumented ... 2. It runs the four programs': says _PGO + ThinLTO + BOLT run in full on every plain make (training build, training run, profile) _ -- **Since 2026-10-07 the profile is made once and kept: the training build and run happen only when build/pgo/satl.profdata is missing or a training program changed, or on `make train`; a plain make compiles the changed object against the profile there is. A plain make also no longer installs (make inst** (make_support/045-optimise.mk lines 13-17 ('ONCE, AND KEPT (2026-10-07)'), rule `$(PGO_PROFILE): $(TRAINING) | $(BUILD_STAMP)` and the `train` target; PROGRESS.md:187-188)
- line 98 ('the 62 word libraries | 0.00%'), line 347 ('What is left plain: the 62 word libraries'), lines 398-399 ('LTO cannot cross a dlopen. Each of the 62 words is its own .so'), line 426 (profiles of word libraries overwrote each other): says _The words are 62 separate .so libraries loaded by dlopen, outside LTO/PGO_ -- **Since 2026-10-07 every word is compiled into satl: satellite-numbers/words/*.cpp (30 files) + word_table.cpp, no dlopen, no libraries target; words/words.tsv has 548 rows. The dlopen/LTO and module-signature points are moot.** (make_support/045-optimise.mk line 20-22 ('EVERY OBJECT IS satl'S OWN SINCE 2026-10-07, the words included'); PROGRESS.md:188; ls satellite-numbers/ -> words/ word_table.cpp word_table.hpp, no per-word folders; wc -l words/words.tsv = 548)
- line 247-248 (Strings): 'satl has not built `.find`/`.replace` yet. When it does, they should sit behind a kernel interface from the start': says _.find and .replace do not exist_ -- **satellite.variable.string.find(x), .replace(a, b) and .split(separator) exist and run, with no kernel interface.** (words/words.tsv rows 82 (1 6 1 3 satellite.variable.string.find(x)), 89 (1 6 1 10 .split(separator)), 91 (1 6 1 12 .replace(a, b)); probe p2_string.satl: exit 0, output lines '10', 'the slow brown fox', '{"the", "quick", "brown", "fox"}', 'true'; sat)

### MILESTONES.md -- GUT (high)

**What it is:** The 2026-09-16 debt list: 'Everything decided and not yet built, one milestone each' (M0.5-M39 plus the author's open decisions), written against PLAN.md and then audited 2026-09-17. Over three weeks it became the place where build records, author rulings, measurements and pasted session notes were appended, so today it is one-third live plan, one-third build history, one-third wrong about the installed satl.

**Why:** Of 1591 lines, roughly 900 describe work that is DONE (M0.5, M0.6, M1-3, M5, M6/7, M8, M14-list, M16, M19, M20.A, M20.D/E/F, M31, M34-rebuild, M36, M37, M38, M39, the struck decisions) and several entries are WRONG about build 0054 (M11 'Not started' while satellite.infinity runs; M18 unmarked though if runs; M20 float 'numbered, not built' though 2 ^ -1 prints 0.5; M26 #13/#16 'still real' though both now fixed; M20.A item 1 return-in-loop 'not fixed' though fixed; threads '1024/256 warm' vs cores x 2; per-word .so libraries and satl-term gone; satellite.access built; 'make installs' reversed 2026-10-07). Lines 79-156 are a pasted /clear session note, not a milestone. NEW_MILESTONES.md (2026-10-06) is the author's current plan. ### PLAN.md -- GUT (high)

**What it is:** The 2026-09-14 order of work for satellite 004 (M0.5-M12), reworked 2026-09-17 when the .satc/.satb/.sati files and the parser died, plus a WITHOUT A MILESTONE list (notes 1-10, to 2026-09-18) of work that lost its owner and of the author's rulings on lists, .reverse() on a float and on an index. PROGRESS.md:5-7 still points readers at it as 'the order of work'.

**Why:** Almost everything in it is now history or wrong about today's satl: M0.5/M0.6 built (and the whole satl-term half of them is dead -- satl-term is gone, make_support/047-window.mk:3-5), M0.7 is a plan for a program that no longer exists, M0.8 is built (session.cpp:202, 440-478; prompt_run.cpp:90), M4/M5/M8/M11/M0 and satellite.access are built, the lists' 'still owed' words run, `for` has a shape, the float type exists, .sate is dropped, the installer installs into ~/.satl, 256 threads became cores x 2, 27 .so libraries became words compiled into satl, 364 words became 548. What is still live is small and recorded thinly elsewhere: combine/M12 (nothing marks a batch yet), M9 polymorph and M10 cxx (no word rows), notes 1 (switch/finally unnumbered), 3 (no entries/), 4 (DESIGN §14 and README still describe the dead files -- TRUE TODAY), 5 (satellite.supertype unnumbered), 9 (float .reverse() DECIDED, not built: FLT-4) and 10 (index .reverse() 'Say which' -- unanswered, and the behaviour is unchanged). Those, the author's quoted words and the measurements must survive; the 900 lines of milestone detail need not.

Part 4B's access/history text is wrong today (access governs satellite.legal's name-ending; history is refused S201, not not_built_yet). Parts 4D and 4F carry rows that 4G superseded (access in a file wrote config.ini; access/infinity at a file's top refused). Parts 4D-4G as a user reference are duplicated, and kept current, by satellite.help/arguments/help_text.txt and PROGRESS.md section 1. ### SATELLITE_WINDOW.md -- GUT (high)

**What it is:** The record of the GTK window in satl: the 2026-09-19 brief, the vendored-GTK measurements (Parts 0-1), what was built on 2026-09-20/21 and 2026-10-03 (Parts 2a/2b/2c: window words, a button that talks back, .open()), the WIN-1..WIN-11 milestones, and Part 3's not-done list. 1,298 lines, written 2026-09-19 to 2026-10-05.

**Why:** About 85% is history that is now wrong or superseded as written: Part 0/1 describe the gtk-4.16.7 build-static stack that 047-window.mk:68-72 says was replaced by vendor/new + build_stack.py (the scripts now live in vendor/gtk-old/); WIN-1, WIN-3, WIN-4, WIN-6, WIN-7, WIN-9, WIN-11 and the .open() are built, yet several section bodies and Part 3 still call them owed; Part 2a's 'a button is the only piece' and Part 2b's 'Only clicked / main's parameter not bound / 004 dlopens each word' are false today (words.tsv:385-415,548; REGISTRY.satellite:368-378; probe p8 exit 0; no dlopen in satellite/). What is still live is small: WIN-2's warm from the token pass, WIN-5 (cairo), WIN-10 (X11), Q-WIN-11b/11c, the 11px/12px font ruling, 'new should not show' (D1/MS-0, not built), the two Part 2c questions, the gio-network sentence, the GPU not ruled -- plus a handful of measured traps and the author's quotes, which must be kept. GTK_AND_NO_DEPENDENCIES.md already carries the vendoring story (Part 0/1, DEP-1..9) and every widget (GTK-0..18); the two help topics are the user-facing reference. Not 'delete': the author's brief, his rulings and several open questions are quoted nowhere else in his words.

### ARCHITECTURES.md -- UPDATE (high)

**What it is:** The author's nine processor names (satl.intel .. satl.lion), their clang -march names and chips, what each -march lets clang use (asked of clang 24), and how the nine relate to M37's 53 builds.

**Why:** Its tables are true today: clang 24 re-asked on 2026-10-07 gives raptorlake ADX+AVX2+BMI2 and no AVX-512, arrowlake/lunarlake AVX-IFMA, cannonlake/tigerlake AVX-512 IFMA, haswell no ADX, skylake ADX -- matching lines 37-45; /proc/cpuinfo here has avx2 and bmi2 and no adx/avx512f on 24 threads (line 56-57); M37 is 0d875d1 with 53 builds (PROGRESS.md:183, MILESTONES.md:1429, 055-cpus.mk CPUS = 53, ls build/cpu = 53). Two things are wrong or missing: the file does not say that the plan it serves was set aside by the author on 2026-09-23 (CPU-PLAN.md header), so a reader takes the nine executables for a live intention when nothing in the tree builds or names them (only haswell_test/satl.haswell, a Sep 23 symlink); and the 'clang's name' column is not the build/cpu/ folder for two of the nine (nehalem is folded into x86-64-v2, lunarlake into arrowlake-s). The quotes and feature table are recorded nowhere else, so it stays as a reference.

**Wrong today:**

- lines 7-8 ('The plan that uses them ... is CPU-PLAN.md') and lines 76-78 ('CPU-PLAN.md proposes replacing the 53 builds and the separate chooser with one satl that picks its own number kernel. Whether the nine names stay as separate executables is a decision the plan leaves open.'): says _CPU-PLAN is a live proposal and the nine-executable question is open under it_ -- **The author set the kernel plan aside on 2026-09-23 ("let's just scrap this whole CPU thing and move on"); nothing was built from it. No satl.<name> executable exists except haswell_test/satl.haswell (a race symlink). The 53 builds and build/satl-cpu-level remain, untouched since Sep 23 except the ch** (CPU-PLAN.md:3-5 (the quote); find . -maxdepth 3 -name 'satl.*' -> ./haswell_test/satl.haswell -> haswell/satl (Sep 23); grep satl\.intel\|satl\.raptor make_support/ Makefile check.sh -> nothing; ls build/cpu | wc -l = 53; ls -la build/satl-cpu-level )
- lines 15 and 22 with lines 73-75: 'M37 builds satl for every processor clang names, into build/cpu/<clang name>/ ... the second column of the first table is the mapping': says _each 'clang's name' is a build/cpu/ folder (nehalem; arrowlake, lunarlake)_ -- **055-cpus.mk folds names that give the same build: nehalem -> x86-64-v2, lunarlake -> arrowlake-s. There is no build/cpu/nehalem or build/cpu/lunarlake; satl.nehalem's build is build/cpu/x86-64-v2/ and satl.lion's are build/cpu/arrowlake/ and build/cpu/arrowlake-s/. (Also 'second column' is the third** (make_support/055-cpus.mk lines 'x86-64-v2 <- corei7 nehalem' and 'arrowlake-s <- lunarlake'; CPUS list has arrowlake, arrowlake-s, x86-64-v2, no nehalem/lunarlake; ls -d build/cpu/nehalem build/cpu/lunarlake -> No such file; build/cpu/x86-64-v2, arro)
- line 73: 'M37 (`0d875d1`) builds satl for every processor clang names': says _every processor clang names_ -- **53 of clang 24's 136 names -- one per distinct instruction set among the 79 64-bit targets; the same file's line 76 says 53, so only this phrase overstates.** (make_support/055-cpus.mk header ('WHICH PROCESSORS, AND WHY 53'); MILESTONES.md:1429-1445 table 136 / 79 / 54 / 53)

**Must survive:** The author's words, lines 3-5 verbatim: "Heres a list of all the architecutres that I want to build an executable for", then "those are the names that I want to use ... as we will be supporting these with these names" (2026-09-23) -- found in no other file. The nine names and their mapping (table lines 12-22). The clang 24 feature table lines 35-45 (re-verified 2026-10-07) and the four conclusions lines 47-59, in particular 'ADX ... is the one thing the 13700K has for numbers that this Xeon lacks'. The Yonah note lines 24-28 (`grep -c lm /proc/cpuinfo` answers 0 on a Yonah). The unanswered question at line 77-78, 'Whether the nine names stay as separate executables', which belongs with CPU-PLAN's 'DECISION OPEN: what happens to make cpus, build/cpu/ ... and the nine satl.<name> executables' -- the author never ruled on it (his set-aside ruling spoke of kernels, not of the nine names).

**Edit plan:** Keep the file. (1) After line 8 add one paragraph: on 2026-09-23 the author set the kernel plan aside (quote CPU-PLAN.md's header verbatim); none of the nine executables was ever built or named in make (the one `satl.haswell` is haswell_test/'s race symlink to build/cpu/haswell/satl); M37's 53 builds in build/cpu/<clang name>/ and build/satl-cpu-level are what exists, and whether they stay is still the author's open decision. (2) In the table lines 12-22 change `satl.nehalem`'s clang name cell to '`nehalem` (built as `x86-64-v2`, the same instruction set)' and `satl.lion`'s to '`arrowlake`, `lunarlake` (built as `arrowlake` and `arrowlake-s`)'. If CPU-PLAN.md is gutted, the open decision on build/cpu/ (53 builds, 4.1 GB) could move here under 'How these relate to M37's 53 builds' so it has a home.

**Overlaps:** MILESTONES.md M37 (lines 1429-1490) and make_support/055-cpus.mk's header cover the 53 builds, the folding of names, and the chooser's rule; satellite/arguments/cpu_facts.hpp and PROGRESS.md:182 cover arguments.cpu.architecture/features ('haswell' = x86-64-v3, the same tier idea). ### DESIGN.md -- UPDATE (high)

**What it is:** The standards every line of satellite-004 is written to (its own words, line 4-5), fixed on 2026-09-14 with the author's rulings marked (author) and that day's measurements marked (measured); 14 sections covering limits, machine codes, the word index, satellite_number, dispatch, start-up threads, names/satellite.log, output, security sweeps, infinity, the prototype's 17 defects, a measurement table, the thread pool/combine plan and the .satc/.satb/.sati file plan. Only §3.4 has been brought up to date (2026-10-07).

**Why:** Most of the body is either 2026-09-14 history (the measurements, the 364-word renumbering, the prototype defects) or wrong about today's satl (config defaults, the row struct, the number struct, the start-up design, the thread pool, combine, every one of .satc/.satb/.sati, infinity's spelling and open questions). But it cannot be gutted or renumbered: 91 comments in the source cite it by section (`grep -rn 'DESIGN §' satellite/ make_support/ satellite-numbers/` = 91, e.g. satellite_number.hpp:21 'DESIGN §4', startup_threads.hpp:9/17 'DESIGN §13'/'DESIGN §1', satellite_log.hpp:21 'DESIGN §7', 047-window.mk:164 'DESIGN 3.4'), PLAN.md cites it 18 times, PROGRESS.md 15, MILESTONES.md 9, README.md:102 and PROGRESS.md:7 send a reader here for 'the standards and every measurement'. The measurements in §5, §6, §8, §12, §13 are recorded nowhere else. So: keep every section number, mark each 2026-09-14 paragraph as that day's design, and add a dated 'Today' paragraph per section as §3.4 already does. If the author would rather DESIGN.md say only what is true today and move the history to PROGRESS.md, that is a gut and his call -- the section numbers would still have to survive as anchors.

**Wrong today:**

- §1.1, lines 15-16: says _Every satellite word is a small compiled C++ library of likely scenarios._ -- **Since 2026-10-07 every word is compiled into satl; nothing is dlopened. §3.4 already says so, §1.1 was not updated.** (make_support/040-sources.mk:25-28 (WORD_SOURCES = $(NUMBERS)/word_table.cpp ...); satellite-numbers/word_table.cpp has 30 rows of {"1 5 1", "satellite.console.display", describe_1_5_1}; satellite-numbers/number_row.hpp:148-150 'Until 2026-10-07 it wa)

### README.md -- UPDATE (high)

**What it is:** The repository's front page: what 004 is, the revision-by-revision history (06..09), the hello-world, the aims, 'where it is today', the build/run lines, what satl runs on, the install, and where 003/002 live.

**Why:** Mostly right and recently touched (the 2026-10-05 console paragraph is current), but the 'aims' and build sections still describe per-word .so libraries, a bare make that installs, and 'string methods are next (M16)' -- all overtaken; the licence count and the make-line comments are off by one build era. Nothing warrants gutting: the revision history is a compact record and the author's 2026-10-05 quotes are here verbatim.

**Wrong today:**

- line 11-12, 'satl --license shows all 26 licences': says _`satl --license` shows all 26 licences that come with that._ -- **`satl --license` lists 27 texts (satellite + 26 other projects, unicode and pcg-cpp among them); `--version` says '26 other projects'.** (run: ~/.satl/satl --license (recipe) exit 0, rows 24 'unicode', 27 'zlib' (WORK/license.txt); ~/.satl/satl --version: 'This binary also carries 26 other projects' (WORK/version.txt); satellite/version/title_lines.hpp:116-120 computes carried-1)
- lines 64-68, first bullet of 'What satellite 004 is being built to be': says _Every word of the language ... is a small compiled C++ library of *likely scenarios*, named by the word's number (`1.5.1.so`) and loaded once at start-up_ -- **Since 2026-10-07 every word is compiled INTO satl; there are no per-word .so files and no `libraries` target; satellite-numbers/ holds words/ + word_table.cpp.** (make_support/050-build.mk:186-188 ('NO `libraries` TARGET SINCE 2026-10-07 ... The script that built a .so a word, satellite-numbers/build_libraries.py, went with them'); ls satellite-numbers: call_number.*, word_table.cpp/hpp, words/ -- no per-word )
- lines 99-100, 'Where it is today': says _`.upper()` and `.lower()` are built; the rest of the string methods (`.size()`, `.contains()` and the others) are next -- MILESTONES M16._ -- **The string methods are built: .size, .contains(x) and the rest are words and run.** (words/words.tsv:80 '1 6 1 1 satellite.variable.string.size', :83 '1 6 1 4 satellite.variable.string.contains(x)'; run WORK/strings.satl exit 0 printed '9', 'true', 'SATELLITE' (WORK/strings.out))

### SCRATCH.md/MISSING_SYNTAX.md -- UPDATE (high)

**What it is:** The author's 2026-10-03 plan for 'missing syntax': his 11 messages verbatim (section 1), the 22 decisions drawn from them (D1-D22), what satl had on build 0001 (section 3), the MISSING_ S-code register (section 4), the report shapes (5), a 15-step tutorial path and its end program (6), 12 open questions Q3-Q14 (7), and 30 milestones MS-1..MS-30 with build/prove notes (8). MS-1..MS-3 carry long AS BUILT / fresh-reader / 'my choices, his to overrule' records.

**Why:** It is a LIVE plan, not history: 27 of its 30 milestones are unbuilt and the code matches the document's own picture of that -- a blank file is still S101 exit 10, a missing return still S103 exit 12, `greet()` never written still S110 exit 13, `satellite.file.copy` is 'no capsule named copy', kMissingProgram..kMissingCapsule are referenced only in s_codes.hpp and missing_cases.cpp, and satellite_window.cpp:229 still calls put_the_frame_up (gtk_window_present at :196) on `new`. MS-1..MS-3 are built exactly as described and PROGRESS.md records none of them under any name, so the AS BUILT notes here are their only prose record. What is wrong is a bounded set: section 3's 'today' rows (labelled build 0001) use the old code names and an `access`-only writable-row rule that no longer holds; five file:line citations have drifted; MS-2's 2026-10-06 note overstates which settings a file may write. ### THREADS.md -- UPDATE (high)

**What it is:** The reference for a satl program's own threads: T1 (satellite.variable.thread t = satellite.thread.new(capsule(args)), .start/.join/.wait/.stop, S720-S728, failure and close rules, the fresh reader's 9 fixes), T2 (the author's lock: off until obj.lock()/f.lock(), writes hold it, S728 for a circle, join lets go of its locks, the second reader's 7 fixes, measurements), T3 (windows on threads, not built).

**Why:** T1 and T2 are built and behave as the document says in every probe (threads.satl 1600 of 1600 and the three display shapes; threads_lock.satl 20000/4000/20001/20002; circle S728 exit 63; window on a thread S727 exit 62; 2 threads x 500 lines into a locked file = 1000 three times; t == u false / t == t true), and T3 is still refused at thread_calls.cpp:297, expression.cpp:1954-1958 and window_methods.cpp:30-34. It is also the only reference for threads: there is no satellite.help topic for threads (ls satellite.help/ -- none), and MILESTONES M38 (line 1498+) defers to it ('THREADS.md has what each word does'). But it is wrong in six places that matter -- the second-join-of-an-object rule, the 1024 warm threads, the config.ini race, the number of tests and rows, the claim that every second-reader fix has a test, and the unlocked-write crash it does not warn about -- and about 45% of it (lines 71-103, 158-203) is review history that belongs in a compact record.

**Wrong today:**

### experiments/nested_include/README.md -- UPDATE (high)

**What it is:** An experiment proving that a bare include name resolves against the directory of the file that wrote the include (a per-file cwd), using a decoy two.satl at the top and the right one beside sub/one.satl; the author's 2026-09-16 question that prompted it.

**Why:** The rule it proves still holds -- rewritten in Revision 07's syntax the tree prints the RIGHT line -- but as written the experiment no longer runs: main.satl's bare `deep()` is refused (an included file's capsules are reached by the file's name since Revision 07), and the runner it names, program_run.cpp, loads the per-word libraries that went on 2026-10-07. check.sh and tests/ have no nested-include row, so this tree is still the only proof of the per-file directory; it needs its three files and two lines updated, not deleting.

**Wrong today:**

- lines 17-19, 'Run it': says _experiments/program_run.cpp  experiments/nested_include/main.satl_ -- **program_run.cpp is a C++ source with no make target, and it loads `build/satellite-numbers` through NumberIndex -- the per-word .so folder that no longer exists. Today the run is `satl experiments/nested_include/main.satl`.** (experiments/program_run.cpp:15-16 (`NumberIndex index; index.load("build/satellite-numbers", state);`); make_support/050-build.mk:186-188 (no libraries since 2026-10-07); grep -n program_run Makefile make_support/*.mk: nothing)
- lines 21-23, 'Expected: three files loaded ... the RIGHT two.satl': says _running main.satl prints 'the RIGHT two.satl -- sub/two.satl, beside the file that included it'_ -- **With the files as they are, satl refuses at the checker: main.satl:6 `deep()` -> S110 'in satellite.main, no capsule named deep', exit 13. Since Revision 07 an included file's capsule is reached as `one.something()`, and 'a file reaches only the files it includes itself', so main cannot call two's c** (run WORK/nested/main.satl (copy of the tree, .sate removed): exit 13, S110 (WORK/nested.out); run with main calling two.deep(): S110 'two is a file that nested2/sub/one.satl includes, and a file reaches only the files it includes itself -- add satell)
- line 21, 'three files loaded (not four -- the decoy is never read)': says _the runner prints how many files were loaded_ -- **satl prints no file count; the 'three not four' assertion has no observable today except that the RIGHT line (not the DECOY line) appears.** (WORK/nested3.out: banner + the one line only)
- the folder (not the README's text): says _main.satl, sub/one.satl, sub/two.satl, two.satl_ -- **The folder also holds main.sate, two.sate, sub/one.sate, sub/two.sate -- leftovers of the .sate bytecode file dropped 2026-10-03.** (find experiments/nested_include -type f lists the four .sate files; ground truth: .sate DROPPED 2026-10-03)

**Must survive:** The author's words, lines 3-4: "does it take includes that are in other .satl files yet? We have to keep a cwd for each file." The rule, lines 12-15: "A bare name resolves against the directory of THE FILE THAT WROTE THE INCLUDE, so `sub/one.satl` must reach `sub/two.satl`. If the per-file directory were ever lost -- resolved against the working directory, or against the main file -- the decoy would win and the program would print the wrong line, loudly." The decoy design itself (two files named two.satl). This rule is checked nowhere else: check.sh has no nested_include/decoy row and tests/ has only missing_include.satl.

**Edit plan:** (1) Update the two source files (the author's call, since it is his experiment): sub/one.satl's one_capsule calls `two.deep()`; main.satl calls `one.one_capsule()` (the shapes that passed in WORK/nested3/). (2) Lines 17-19: 'Run it: satl experiments/nested_include/main.satl (to a file or with SATL_NO_WINDOW set)'. (3) Lines 21-23: 'Expected: exit 0 and the one line "the RIGHT two.satl -- ..."; the DECOY line means the per-file directory was lost.' Add: 'Since Revision 07 (2026-09) a file reaches only the files it includes itself, by name -- so two's capsule is reached from sub/one.satl, not from main.' (4) Offer to delete the four .sate files. (5) Consider a check.sh row or a tests/ program built from this tree, so the rule is checked and not only demonstrated.

### licenses/README.md -- UPDATE (high)

**What it is:** The index of licenses/: one verbatim text per vendored project, why there are so many, the three elections (freetype FTL, cairo LGPL-2.1, glib LGPL-2.1-or-later), the version table, pcg-cpp's special case, the mixed-licence audit findings, repairs, and two unsettled questions (gperf, meson).

**Why:** The elections, the table of versions (every row matches vendor/new/) and the audit record are sound and recorded nowhere else. But the FreeType credit it calls 'the one open item' has been discharged in --version/--help, the counts in the headings are two eras old, the SATELLITE_WINDOW.md line reference moved, and -- the one thing the author needs to hear -- the installed binary carries VTE, lz4, simdutf and fast_float strings while licenses/ has no folder and `satl --license` no row for any of them.

**Wrong today:**

- lines 51-53, blockquote under 'freetype -- ELECTED': says _That obligation is not yet discharged and is the one open item in this folder._ -- **The FTL credit sentence is printed by `satl --version` and `satl --help`: 'Portions of this software are copyright (c) 2026 The FreeType Project (https://freetype.org).  All rights reserved.'** (satellite/version/title_lines.hpp:124-131 ('THE ONE MANDATORY CREDIT SENTENCE IN THE WHOLE SET'); run ~/.satl/satl --version (WORK/version.txt) last two lines)
- line 15 heading and line 20: says _Why there are twenty-five of these and not two / `make GTK=vendor` compiles twenty-four other projects in_ -- **licenses/ holds 27 folders (25 projects + satellite + unicode, pcg-cpp among the 25); the binary says 26 other projects; `satl --license` lists 27. The stack is built by `make window` and GTK=vendor is the default, so a plain `make` carries it.** (ls licenses: 27 directories (cairo .. zlib, satellite, unicode); WORK/version.txt '26 other projects'; WORK/license.txt rows 1-27; make_support/047-window.mk:64 'GTK ?= vendor', :306 'window:')
